| R58 | **Fourth full audit pass — and the one finding that mattered is a shape the previous three could not see: a guard that degrades *safely* but *silently*.** Lanes chosen by an `optimization-engines` run over a fully computable spec (zero model turns): `android-app-auditor` + `code-reviewer` + `code-verification-core` + `claude-automation-recommender`, scope "changed since R35", fitness 0.859. What the search deselected is as much the point as what it kept: `coderabbit` scored **feasibility 0** (CLI not installed, requires an auth login and sends diffs to an external API — a user action, not mine); `code-modernization` scored **fit 0.15** (this tree is Kotlin 2.1 / compileSdk 36 / Compose / KSP, there is no legacy to uplift); the official `/code-review` scored **novelty 0.15** (HEAD merged, tree clean, so the diff it would read is empty); `code-simplifier` was excluded as the only *mutating* lane in a request for an audit. The component that changed the ranking was **marginal_value = fit × novelty**: `android-app-auditor` is a perfect premise match and yet worth little here — three prior full passes (R14/R21/R35, the last 0 CRITICAL / 0 HIGH) against only **82 lines** of manifest and Gradle changed since — while `code-reviewer` covers a surface where **44 of 86 source files** changed, +2520/-998. Scoring how well a tool fits without scoring how much has moved since it last ran would have ranked the two backwards. | ✅ **Resolved.** **HIGH — a denied notification permission was invisible in every surface of the app.** `EmivitaApp.kt:129` asks for POST_NOTIFICATIONS exactly once and never again (correct: PERM-023/024 say respect the decision, do not pressure it), and `postIfPermitted` then drops each notification with a `Log.w`. Nothing in the tree called `areNotificationsEnabled()`. So a user who declined once, or who switched notifications off in system Settings a month later, had an app with a populated Today screen and alarms genuinely armed in `dumpsys alarm` that would never post a reminder again. What makes it a defect rather than a choice is that the app had **already solved this twice, ten lines apart**: `FullScreenAlarmAccessCard` and `BatteryOptimizationCard` are passive status cards for the two *weaker* silent failures, and the strongest one had none. Fixed as a third card in the same block, first of the three because it is the only one meaning nothing arrives at all. It also covers a mode no permission check can see — a tier at `IMPORTANCE_NONE`, so an Alarm-tier medicine "that must not be skipped" posts to a channel the user blocked. Only `NONE` counts: a tier merely quietened is a preference, and warning about it would be the nagging PERM-024 names. `ReminderNotificationChannels.blockedTiers()` walks `NotificationPriority.entries` rather than a hand-written id list, so a new tier is covered by construction. Decision logic split into pure functions over plain values (`NotificationDelivery.kt`) for the same reason `EmergencyCardExporter.buildLines` is split from its PDF writer — 6 JVM tests, no Robolectric. **MEDIUM — `ProjectionState` could not say whether it was complete.** When `COMPUTE_BUDGET_MILLIS` stops a pass, `OccurrenceProjectionCache` publishes what it has, which is deliberate; being *indistinguishable from a whole answer* was not. A medicine absent from `stockRunOutDates` meant "will not run out in the window" **and** "was never reached" — one value standing for two facts, which is R56's `error` overload in another costume. Now carries `truncated`, and logs how many of how many medicines it visited. **Two stale claims, both the R47/R56 class.** `ReminderScheduling.kt`'s KDoc still said weekend mode applies "unconditionally, no per-reminder opt-out" — untrue since R36 gated it on `Reminder.weekendModeOptIn`, so one file answered the same question two opposite ways. `AndroidManifest.xml` justified removing `REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS` with "nothing checks isIgnoringBatteryOptimizations" — untrue since R39's `BatteryOptimizationCard`. **The removal is still correct**, and that is precisely why the stale reason survived: nobody re-reads the justification for a decision that is right. Both corrected to the reason that survives. **A finding I raised and then withdrew, recorded because the mistake is instructive.** I reported that weekend mode silently *drops* doses from the projection, with a measurement behind it: a 2-hourly reminder across an 08:00–20:00 Saturday window yields 7 occurrences where an ordinary day yields 13. The measurement was real; the interpretation was wrong. `ReminderAlarmReceiver.kt:145` reschedules from `scheduledEpochMillis + 60_000L` — the *fired* time, which is the pushed one — so the device's own alarm chain performs exactly the walk `occurrencesFor` performs. The six intermediate doses are never armed and never materialize a DoseEvent on a real phone either. **The projection is a faithful simulation, not a lossy one.** What survives is a documentation item, now in `applyWeekendMode`'s KDoc: on a sub-daily reminder weekend mode does not delay a dose, it *skips* every dose inside the window and resumes after, which "delays a dose" (§9, and the R36 migration's backfill argument) describes only for FixedTime. The general lesson is rule 9's twin: **a number measured correctly still has to be interpreted against what the system does, not against what you expected it to do.** **Automation, since two of the four lanes kept pointing at process rather than code.** `.gitattributes` added — R57 recorded CRLF turning a three-line edit into a 990-line diff, and commit 71b9a76 is the *cleanup* for that with no *prevention*; `core.autocrlf=false` was set on one machine and travels with nobody. Keystore, APK and archive types declared `binary` explicitly rather than left to `text=auto`'s guess, since a wrong guess there rewrites bytes inside the shared signing identity. Verified to renormalize zero existing files. `CLAUDE.md` added, against §10's "one file" rule and worth naming as such: the rule bars a second document competing for findings and rationale, and this carries neither — only the commands from §10 that an agent will not find 185 KB into SPEC.md, and a line saying SPEC.md wins wherever they disagree. Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **166 tests / 0 failures** (158 → 160 → 166) from a cleared results directory, `lintDebug` green |  |
| R59 | **Device pass for §9 #19 Stages A and B, and the four defects the owner found by using the phone that no test and no en-XA sweep had reached.** Scope chosen by an `optimization-engines` run over a fully computable spec (zero model turns; fitness 0.869 after two scorer corrections, both disclosed: `talkback` had been coupled to the ledger's effort and `owner_fit` gave no credit for spending the owner's minutes on the two checks only a human can make). Selected: en-XA by agent screenshots (`cmd locale set-app-locales`, `screencap`, Read the PNGs), ar-XB in, the real accessibility tree via `uiautomator dump` per screen, TalkBack by the owner's ear, the R58 notification states made in system Settings, the OLED/texture/splash checks by the owner's eye, a ledger row. Deselected with reasons: the Espresso accessibility gate (`espresso-accessibility` is not in the offline cache; a follow-up, not a failure), a re-run of the §9 #20 suite (code unchanged since 5/5 — later re-run anyway once the alarm channel changed), the `dist/vc1` upgrade path. | ✅ **Resolved**, owner checklist partly open. **What the automated half found.** en-XA rendered **seven** plain-English strings the sink-based gate structurally cannot see (commits 4d2d9f1, e5251aa): the three theme-mode labels derived from `enum.name`, a formatter's fourth argument, the R58 card's title and body, and the `"dose"` unit fallback — every one a literal assigned before it reached a sink, exactly the blind spot the gate's own KDoc names. Its glyph traps are worth recording so the next sweep does not re-learn them: en-XA maps b→ɓ, f→ƒ, v→V and x→Cyrillic х, so a naive "is this accented?" match reported the R58 card as absent when the screenshot showed it present in plain English — the false NONE was mine, the leftover was real. `uiautomator dump` confirmed the nodes the ten Robolectric tests assert exist in Android's own tree with the same roles, states and descriptions (dose rows `checkable`/`stateDescription`, calendar cells with per-status counts, the biometric row as one `Switch`, radio rows as one node each); `onClickLabel` is not surfaced in a dump, so that half stays Robolectric-only. ar-XB: no clipped or overlapping text on the four tabs at 30% expansion; RTL mirrors correctly. R58 states: revoking `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` produced the "can't reach you" card, re-granting removed it, on the same screen without restart (captures `50_`–`53_`). **What the owner found — four defects, each a shape the tests could not hold.** (1) *The Medicines header was a different, taller component* than the other three tabs: a Scaffold/TopAppBar where the others use a plain Row. Replaced with the same header; the first fix still sat 34 px low because an `IconButton`'s 48 dp minimum target stretched the row, so the button now floats in a Box and the list padding matches; measured in the dump at 144/144 header and 281/281 first-card top against Today, not eyeballed. (2) *No medicine could be given a priority.* `Medicine.notificationPriority` existed from the first schema and picked the channel every reminder posts to, and no screen ever set it — every medicine was STANDARD, so the High and Alarm tiers, `AlarmActivity`, and the `FullScreenAlarmAccessCard` that explains it were **unreachable from the UI**. A section in the medicine form now, one selectable row per tier using the same name and description resources as the Notifications & Alerts screen and the channels themselves. Found by a person expecting an alarm and getting a chime; no test asserts that a column is *settable*. (3) *The Due row squeezed the medicine name to three characters* beside two labelled chips — "Ser / tral / ine". Chips moved to their own line. The chips only render for a PENDING dose and Robolectric asserts semantics, not pixels. (4) *The Alarm tier did not ring and was not full screen.* Diffed against medTimer's `ReminderAlarmActivity`: this port had `setShowWhenLocked`/`setTurnScreenOn` and stopped. medTimer hides the system bars, loops the alarm ringtone through a `MediaPlayer` on `USAGE_ALARM` (the stream that rings through vibrate mode and, by default, DND) and vibrates 500/500 until answered; the port had none of it. And `dumpsys notification` showed the "Alarm" channel had been created with the defaults — `mSound=notification_sound usage=USAGE_NOTIFICATION vibration=false` — a notification channel wearing an alarm's name. A channel's sound is fixed the moment it is first created, so the fix is a new id `reminders_alarm_v2` with the alarm tone on `USAGE_ALARM` and vibration, the legacy channel deleted (`mDeleted=true` confirmed), `CATEGORY_ALARM` set, and the `VIBRATE` permission the manifest never had. `theAlarmTierChannelIsActuallyAnAlarm` now pins the three channel properties on the device — nothing on the JVM can see what a channel was created with, which is precisely why this shipped. Not a defect: on Android 14 a full-screen intent becomes a heads-up banner while the screen is on and unlocked; the activity launches only when the phone is off or locked, and medTimer is subject to the same rule. **Still open from the owner's checklist:** hearing the Alarm tier ring; the TalkBack walk (Today row, calendar, biometric switch, radio rows, palette cards); the six palettes at low brightness in daylight; the R55 chart texture at 6 dp after a taken dose; the splash flash; blocking the Alarm channel alone to see the "one tier is blocked" card. Flagged, not fixed: `MedicineDetailScreen` still uses the tall Scaffold header the Medicines tab just lost; battery optimisation is on for Emivita on the test phone (the R39 card is showing there, correctly); Glance widget semantics. **Lesson, rule 18 below:** the four owner findings were all *reachability* and *layout* — a settable column nobody could set, a component nobody compared, a row nobody rendered at phone width, a channel nobody inspected — and every one was invisible to a suite that asserts behaviour on nodes it constructs itself. Verified: JVM `EXIT=0`, **178 tests / 0 failures** (166 → 178) from a cleared results directory, `lintDebug` green; device `NotificationDeliveryDeviceTest` 3/0/0 on LE2123 |  |
| R60 | **Code-level diff against medTimer v1.24.6 — 285 feature rows, every one cited on both sides.** §5 was a category-level reuse map, and R59 showed what that granularity hides: a row marked "ported" with the half that rings missing. Method chosen by an `optimization-engines` run over a fully computable spec (15,552 points, 3,888 feasible, enumerated exhaustively — the engine's own search reached 0.898, the exact optimum is 0.907): compare by feature row then diff paired functions; evidence by citation *plus* medTimer's own scheduler tests transliterated onto Emivita's engine; six Opus 5 subagents by area with ≥2 claims per area re-read against source by me; the ranked as-is list with per-function diffs; parity tests and the eight gate-blind literals landed in the repo; recorded here; MIT notice at first import; adoption the owner's call. Two tie-cluster calls (top four within 0.005) decided by argument and disclosed: no `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` until something is actually copied (a notice for a dependency the tree does not have is the R47/R56 stale-claim class), and the five literals included despite losing by 0.006 on a two-minute overrun of a 360-minute budget constant. Deselected: porting `SchedulingSimulatorTest` (budget-bound; +0.003 evidence for −0.016 effort — a follow-up), `BackupFieldParityTest` (an *adoption* candidate, E-16), inline execution (2× wall-clock for a risk delta spot-verification covers). **Spot-verification: 17 claims across the six areas re-read against source, 0 wrong.** | ✅ **Resolved** for the diff, the three scheduling defects and the eight strings; the adoption list is with the owner (§9 #21). **Tally** (Appendix E): 285 rows — 92 inspiration, 73 equal, 51 Emivita-better, 42 medTimer-better, 23 n/a, **4 import-as-is** (`shouldBeSilent` chime suppression B-06, `getMedicineName` masking B-39, `CalendarHelper` run-out calendar intent C-20, `ChartSeriesColors` D-26 — every other candidate is typed on medTimer's entities, Hilt, or `PreferencesDataSource`). **§5 corrected in one large way** (§5.1): medTimer's UI is no longer "Fragments, XML — not portable"; its medicine list, Statistics screen, `SortableTable` and theme are Compose, and the medicine list has the drag-reorder Emivita has a `sortOrder` column for and no UI. **The instrument that made "is Emivita an improvement" a count rather than an opinion:** thirty of medTimer's `schedulertests` cases ported by meaning (medTimer derives "next" from event history, Emivita from `notBefore` plus an anchor). Run first against the unfixed code: **21 parity, 4 documented divergences asserted as Emivita's behaviour with their Appendix E row, 4 failures — each for the reason the medTimer test names**, plus one regression guard for the fix. **HIGH — an ON_RESOLVED interval or window was re-armed while a dose was still unanswered.** `SchedulingEngine` anchored on `getLastResolved` (which skips PENDING) and never asked whether a dose was outstanding, so `rescheduleAll()` at boot, or any edit of the reminder, armed a second alarm at `previousResolution + k·interval` and materialised a second PENDING dose beside the first. medTimer's `IntervalScheduling` returns null in exactly this state, with the comment "otherwise the notification loop would fire continuously"; its ported test failed here with the second slot armed. Fixed on the alarm path; the projection walk instead assumes the outstanding dose resolves at its slot, as medTimer's simulator does, so the run-out date does not jump while a dose sits unanswered. **HIGH — a late-delivered alarm replayed every missed slot.** `ReminderAlarmReceiver` rescheduled from the fired slot + 1 min with no floor and `AlarmScheduler` arms a past instant, which AlarmManager fires at once: a clock set forward three days chained every missed dose as a notification. medTimer bounds catch-up to today (`raisedToday`, `adjustToToday`). Floor at now in `RealSchedulingCoordinator.scheduleOrCancel`, the one point every caller passes. **MEDIUM — a ONCE expiration alert never re-armed for a new pack.** Nothing cleared `lastAlertNotifiedEpochDay` when a later expiry was entered; the recorded day now blocks only inside the current lead window. **Two documentation defects:** `ReminderConfig` justified the cyclic pattern's before-anchor behaviour as "matching medTimer's own documented behavior" — medTimer's `StandardScheduling` does the opposite and its test pins it; kept as a deliberate divergence, justification corrected. §5 called the stock sentinel "adopted verbatim" — same type and default, but medTimer writes `stockBefore` at raise time and never reads the pair for undo, Emivita writes it at resolution and undo depends on it (C-08). **Eight gate-blind literals** (Stage A part 4): the notification body itself, the alarm placeholder, the stock widget subtitle, the stock screen's run-out line, two save errors, the wrong-password message — every one assigned to a `val` or state first, none rendered by R59's sweep because each needs an error path, a cold widget, or a run-out. **Verified defects left for the owner, all S-sized, none fixed here because each changes behaviour beyond what a diff was asked for** (Appendix E, per-area Findings): Standard/High reminder and both alert notifications have no content intent — tapping them does nothing (B); snooze and nag alarm slots are keyed per reminder, so two unanswered doses of one reminder share a slot and the second snooze cancels the first (B); no `rescheduleAll()` on app launch, so after a user force-stop reminders stay dead until reboot or an edit (B); the engine's zone is captured once in a lazy singleton and nothing handles `TIMEZONE_CHANGED`/`TIME_SET` (B); a dose reminder cannot be deleted, paused or disabled from any screen — `ReminderRepository.delete()` and `setEnabled()` have no caller outside the alert toggles (C/D); correcting a Missed dose to Taken with a different amount decrements stock by the *old* amount because `resolve()` runs before `editDoseAmount()` (C); "Refill now" on a medicine with no saved unit inserts a REFILLED row and changes nothing (C); `lockRemindersUntilTakenByDefault` is persisted, read, and settable from nowhere (D); backup restore uses the default `Json`, so a file from a newer Emivita fails on the first unknown key and `schemaVersion` is neither written nor checked (E); Medical ID edits never trigger auto-sync — `ProfileRepository.update` is the one mutation without `notifyChanged()` (E); a null SAF stream on history export writes nothing and says nothing, the shape R23 fixed for backup (E); the History table sorts by scheduled time while displaying resolved time (D); the CSV row pairs the scheduled date with the resolved time, a timestamp that never happened (E); hard-coded `HH:mm`/`M/d` patterns and a locale-blind `formatDecimal` that Stage C will expose (D/E/F); the production date and `Medicine.notes` are columns no screen can set (C). **Lesson, rule 19 below.** Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **208 tests / 0 failures** (178 → 208) from a cleared results directory, `lintDebug` green; `origin/master` 0/0 after push |  |
| R61 | **The verified-defects batch from the medTimer diff (§9 #22) — twenty S-sized findings R60 listed and left for the owner, chosen by the owner as the first of four batches.** Built in five groups, each its own commit, the §10 triad after each. | ✅ **Resolved.** **Alarms and receivers** (`3367c00`): snooze and nag alarm slots were keyed per reminder, so the two unanswered doses an ON_REMINDED interval reminder can hold shared one slot and the second snooze cancelled the first — keyed per dose event now, as medTimer's `PendingIntentFactory` does, with a Robolectric test on `ShadowAlarmManager` that sees the real collision (five cases, including the adversarial `reminderId == doseEventId`); Standard/High reminders and both alert notifications had no content intent, so a tap did nothing — `openAppPendingIntent` on every kind; nothing re-armed alarms after a user force-stop until a reboot, a restore or an edit — `AppContainer.rescheduleOnLaunch()` from `MainActivity.onCreate`; the zone was captured once at construction of three lazy singletons and no receiver handled `TIMEZONE_CHANGED`/`TIME_SET` — `fixedZone` is a test seam and production reads `ZoneId.systemDefault()` on every use, and `TimeChangeReceiver` re-arms from now. A pre-R61 install may hold per-reminder snooze/nag alarms; they fire once, find the dose resolved or the reminder gone, and stop. **Stock** (`ac64116`): correcting a Missed dose to Taken with a different amount decremented stock by the *old* amount, because `resolve()` takes the change from the stored `doseAmount` and `editDoseAmount()` never touches stock — the amount lands first now, a Compose test pins the order with `coVerifyOrder`; "Refill now" on a medicine with no saved unit inserted a REFILLED row and changed nothing — `logRefill` returns null and inserts nothing for an untracked medicine, the button is gated on a saved unit and says why. **Data** (`8ef14d2`): the default `Json` rejected a backup from a newer build at its first unknown key and never wrote `schemaVersion` — the field guarding the format was on no file; one `BackupJson` (`ignoreUnknownKeys`, `encodeDefaults`) for export, auto-sync and restore, and `decodeBackup` refuses a newer schema by version number (four tests: written, tolerated, refused, pre-R61 file read as v1); `ProfileRepository.update` was the one mutation without `autoSyncManager.notifyChanged()`, so Medical ID edits reached the sync folder only after some unrelated change; history export used `?.use { }` on the SAF stream and said nothing either way — the R23 shape — both exports now fail loudly and report either outcome; the CSV paired the scheduled date with the resolved time, a timestamp that never happened for a 23:30 dose taken at 00:10 — `Date,Time` are the scheduled slot and a `Recorded` column carries the answer, blank while unanswered (§9 #10's column set changes; two tests). **Display and locale** (`0d9efec`): every screen, both widgets and the notification hard-coded `HH:mm` and English date patterns, so a 12-hour-locale user saw "8:00 PM" in the notification and "20:00" on Today for the same dose, and a pattern string is the one thing the pseudolocale sweep cannot see — `LocaleFormats` routes times through `ofLocalizedTime(SHORT)` and dates through `DateFormat.getBestDateTimePattern` skeletons; `formatDecimal` was locale-blind and `toDoubleOrNull()` rejected the Decimal keyboard's own separator under it/de — `formatDecimalLocalized`/`parseDecimal` at every dose field, invariant first so `0.5` means a half everywhere, the exports deliberately unchanged (a CSV cell is data); the History table sorted by the scheduled slot while displaying the resolved time; the History view mode reset on every navigation — persisted by enum name. Six Robolectric cases under en-US and it-IT. **Settable columns** (`60e39d0`): `lockRemindersUntilTakenByDefault` had a key, a flow, a setter and readers and no screen called the setter, so the medicine form's "use global default" always inherited false — a switch on Notifications & Alerts; `productionDateEpochDay` was carried forward on every save — a `DateField`. **Not in this batch, by design:** `Medicine.notes` (wire as §6's inline section or drop in the next migration — the owner's call, asked with the reminder-management questions); the per-reminder enable/delete UI, which is §9 #24's first item rather than a defect fix. Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **227 tests / 0 failures** (208 → 227) from a cleared results directory, `lintDebug` green; the device suite (§9 #20) is due a re-run because the alarm request codes changed — asked, not assumed |  |
| R62 | **Cross-app pass — the diff exercise run against every other similar app worth reading, so the feature batches are picked against the field and not against one reference.** The owner's request after R60. Candidates discovered by search (F-Droid, GitHub topics): HomeMedkit, MediTrak, RxDroid, Did I Take My Meds?, Simpill, Daily Pill, Pill Time, Dosage (Linux), and the Medisafe/MyTherapy store listings. **Every one with source is GPL** — medTimer's MIT was the exception — so the legal outputs are inspiration and behaviour-derived tests, never copied code; the owner chose *inspiration only* over relicensing Emivita GPL-3. Selection by an `optimization-engines` run over a fully computable spec (36,864 points, 24,528 feasible, enumerated exhaustively; the engine's search reached 0.834, the exact optimum 0.849): HomeMedkit, MediTrak, Did I Take My Meds?, Daily Pill, Pill Time at delta depth (rows only for what neither Emivita nor medTimer has), Dosage and the listings read online, behaviour-derived tests, one Appendix F, six Opus 5 subagents with spot-verification. Deselected: RxDroid (archived 2026-07-23, its README sends users to medTimer; −0.011), Simpill (2023; −0.017). Top three within 0.004 differed only on the free listings and the tests — both in, by argument. Five archives downloaded with the owner's explicit yes (7.3 MB total, `Downloads/xapps/`). **Six claims spot-verified against the trees, none wrong** (HomeMedkit's obfuscated barcode endpoint re-derived from its `shiftCipher`; MediTrak's dose-count course at `DatabaseController.cpp:707-726`; Did I Take My Meds?'s slot claim at `Medication.kt:422-432`; Daily Pill's `setShowWhenLocked` dialog; Pill Time's `maxDose` display-only; HomeMedkit's five `contentDescription`s, all null). | ✅ **Resolved**; adoption re-ranked in Appendix F.5 (E.4 v2). **154 delta rows: 79 novel, 26 app-better, 28 Emivita-better, 9 excluded by design, 7 equal.** What the field has that neither Emivita nor medTimer does, and that survives §1: a *medicine cabinet* model (HomeMedkit — dosage-form catalogue with per-form unit and icon, kits, expiry-sorted list with hide-empty and colour-coded expired/empty cards, month/year expiry picker, package-opened date, full-text search); a timestamped per-medication notes journal and an auto change-log on every edit (MediTrak — the adverse-effects record §1's clinician export has no slot for); a dose-count-limited course and an early-take window (MediTrak); a per-medicine PRN ceiling that is *displayed, never enforced*, with "headroom returns at …" and time-since-last as the primary display (Pill Time, Did I Take My Meds?); a quiet lock-screen answer without unlocking and a reminder that expires at day end (Daily Pill); missed doses back-filled into history for days the app was not running, day-end auto-confirm with a distinct status, as-needed medicines as standing rows on Today, an aggregate low-stock digest with a tab badge (Dosage); a doctor report over a chosen range with a per-medicine filter, and a time-zone travel choice (Medisafe). **Where Emivita is ahead of all of them:** alarm sound/looping/vibration and the full-screen tier; stock arithmetic with a floor and history-preserving undo; boot recovery (HomeMedkit re-arms only *overdue* alarms, hence its "Fix notifications" button); expiry alerts with lead and once/daily; hand-written migrations with a test each; encryption; app lock; the three-tier channels; accessibility (HomeMedkit: five `contentDescription`s, all null, zero `semantics`; Emivita: 27 semantics blocks, 8 `stateDescription`, 28 `Role` uses); and tests at all (HomeMedkit and Daily Pill have none, MediTrak four). **Findings on Emivita** (Appendix F.3): a Taken dose whose amount is edited afterwards leaves stock decremented by the old amount — R61 fixed the Missed→Taken order, not this (PT-F1, S); Standard/High reminders carry no `setPublicVersion`, so a redacting lock screen shows "Content hidden" and no actions (DP-F1, S); channel names stay in the old language after a system language change until the process restarts (HM-F3, S; invisible until Stage C); an expiry alert fires for an empty pack (HM-F1, S, judgement); an armed alarm suppressed by the OS is repaired silently on launch where the app has the data to say one was (DP-F2, S); and two decisions now on record as decisions rather than omissions — never-fired doses are absent from adherence (DS-F1) and PRN logging gives no recent-dose signal (PT-F2). Three behaviour-derived tests pin where Emivita stands on the PRN ceiling, the empty-pack expiry alert, and the notification's content intent / timeout / public version, each naming the Appendix F row that would flip it. `Medicine.notes` wired at the owner's call (R61's open item). Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **230 tests / 0 failures** (227 → 230), `lintDebug` green; device suite **6/0/0** on LE2123 after R61's alarm-slot change |  |
| R63 | **§9 #24 reminder management — the first of the owner's three feature batches, built to the owner's UI answers.** Asked before building (memory: layout and navigation are settled with the owner): pause = a switch on the detail row and delete = a button at the bottom of the edit form (chosen over an overflow per row or both-in-the-form); sort keys manual / name / next dose / expiry-and-stock; card state taken-not-taken, expired-or-empty tint with a hide-empty filter, course state line, and the owner's own addition, "about to run out". | ✅ **Resolved.** **Piece 1** (`1bddd85`): until now the only way to be rid of a dose reminder was deleting its medicine (Appendix E C-29 / D F1) — `ReminderRepository.setEnabled` and `delete` had existed since R25 with no caller outside the alert toggles. The detail row is two nodes on purpose: the row is a Button that opens the editor, the Switch is its own labelled node ("Fixed Time reminder enabled") that pauses — two actions, unlike the Stage B rows where one action had two nodes; an alert reminder gets no switch (the stock screen owns it) and a paused row says "Paused". "Delete reminder" reaches all six forms through the shared `ReminderFormScaffold`, edit path only, with a confirm that says what happens to a following reminder (switched off, §7 R25). The overflow gains Duplicate and Duplicate with reminders (§6 planned the pair; every neighbour has it, F HM-41 / E C-28): the copy keeps every field but the barcode (a code identifies one pack) and stock (a copy starts untracked), takes the same tags, and re-creates each reminder through the repository so it gets its own alarm, with LinkedFollowing parents remapped to the copies — a child whose parent was not copied is dropped rather than left pointing at the original. Pause/Resume all leaves the two alert types alone: pausing the doses does not stop the pack expiring. **Piece 2** (`ccb2122`): the Medicines tab re-read as medicine-with-reminders per profile (a new `@Transaction` query), the four sort keys persisted by enum name, and a hand-rolled drag on a handle in manual order — `detectDragGestures`, the dragged card's centre crossing a neighbour re-orders the local list, drop writes one row's `sortOrder` as the midpoint of its new neighbours (medTimer `move()`'s idea, MIT, stated in a sentence and re-implemented as `sortOrderForMove`; nothing renumbered). The `reorderable` library is not in the offline cache and F HM-03 showed 176 lines suffice. Every handle carries "Move up" / "Move down" as custom accessibility actions, since a screen reader cannot drag — the first custom actions in the app, which §9 #19 had deferred. The card state is a pure function (`cardStateFor`, `courseStateFor`, `sortEntries`) over the medicine, its reminders, today's dose events and the projection: DUE beats DONE, DONE needs nothing projected later today, a PRN row is not today's scheduled answer, empty means tracked-and-zero, expired is strictly before today, runs-out only within seven days; a future start wins over an end date wins over a cycle day; NEXT_DOSE puts unprojected medicines last in manual order, EXPIRY_STOCK puts empty first then soonest run-out then soonest expiry. Tint for empty or expired is `attention`, never `error` (§6's register). One merged Button node per card with the state line as its `stateDescription`. Hide-empty is shown only when something is empty. 16 JVM cases for the rules, 6 for duplicate/pause-all, 6 Compose semantics cases. **Not done, by design:** the NEXT_DOSE sort reads the projection cache's window, so a medicine whose next dose lies beyond 28 days sorts last with the unscheduled; the drag has no haptic; F HM-40's unsaved-changes guard is §9 #25/#26 territory. Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **246 tests / 0 failures** (230 → 246), `lintDebug` green |  |
| R64 | **§9 #25 notification polish — the second owner-picked batch, built to three answers asked first:** a swiped reminder records what a three-way setting says (Nothing / Snooze / Skip), **default Snooze** — the owner's choice over medTimer's default Skip, "a swipe is closer to *not now* than to *no*"; no day-end expiry (Daily Pill DP-02 declined: an unanswered dose is what the notification is for); no DND override (§1's posture kept — the Alarm tier already rings through DND on the alarm stream). All four item groups picked. | ✅ **Resolved.** **Piece A** (`3175182`): `setDeleteIntent` routes a swipe to the receiver, which reads the setting *at swipe time*, Skip respecting the lock the button respects; every reminder carries a nameless public version ("Medication reminder — a dose is due. Unlock to see which.") with the same actions, so a redacting lock screen shows Take/Snooze instead of "Content hidden" (F PT-06 / DP-F1 — until now only the ALARM tier reached the lock screen); the body says "N left after this dose" when stock is tracked (F HM-18, text only, never HomeMedkit's take-blocking); reminders inside ten seconds alert once, the ALARM tier exempt (E B-06 — medTimer's `shouldBeSilent`, re-implemented from the description rather than copied, so no notice file yet); names can be hidden in every notification (§9 #11, `maskedMedicineName`). The cross-app test that pinned "no public version" flipped, as it was written to. **Piece B** (`ccb996e`): the Alarm tier's tone is the user's through the system picker with medTimer's double fallback (chosen → default) when it cannot be opened; two switches respect the phone's silent mode for sound and for vibration — `USAGE_ALARM` rings through it by design otherwise; a `setAlarmClock` opt-in arms the regular slot as a user-visible alarm (F HM-21: status-bar icon, lock-screen time, less deferral), mirrored onto `AlarmScheduler` by the container and re-armed when it flips; "Send a test reminder" posts a real Standard-channel notification with nothing to take (F DP-05); a `LOCALE_CHANGED` receiver re-registers the channels so their names follow a language change (F HM-F3). **Piece C** (`873d164`): `rescheduleAll` re-arms the future (R61) and left the past alone — a dose that fired before a reboot or a force-stop and was never answered had no notification until now. `UnansweredDoseReposter` re-posts the last day's unanswered scheduled doses whose notification is not showing, after boot and on launch (E B-20, medTimer's `Autostart`); three Robolectric cases on `ShadowNotificationManager` pin the rules, including that a launch cannot re-chime a reminder still showing. And a per-reminder pre-alarm lead — **DB v6 → v7**, `reminders.preAlarmMinutes INTEGER` nullable, `MigrationFrom6To7Test` — a silent "coming up" notification N minutes ahead in its own alarm slot, armed with the regular one only when its instant is still ahead, cancelled with it, replaced by the real reminder (F HM-19). **Piece D** (`2363269`): every reminder joins one notification group; from two showing, a silent summary carries "Take all" over exactly the dose ids that are showing, rebuilt after every post and every dismissal, gone when one is left — platform grouping rather than medTimer's single combined notification, so each reminder keeps its own buttons; an ask-each-time switch turns the Snooze action into `SnoozePickerActivity`, a dialog-themed activity (a notification action cannot show a dialog) offering 5/15/30/60 or a number, making the same `scheduleSnooze` call. **A §10 rule caught by the build, not by me:** the manifest comment for that activity contained `--` and the merger refused it — the rule exists because the parser enforces it. **Left open:** the pre-alarm has no Take action (it is a heads-up, not a dose); the group summary's count is doses, not medicines; DND override stays declined. Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **259 tests / 0 failures** (246 → 259), `lintDebug` green; device suite **6/0/0** on LE2123 over the v7 migration and the new receivers, debug build reinstalled |  |
| R65 | **§9 #26 history as a record — the third owner-picked batch, built to four answers asked first:** doses the app never fired become **Missed** rows on launch and boot (over the alternative of leaving them absent); the day-end auto-confirm was declined in favour of keeping *Taken at fire time* as the only automatic answer; a deleted medicine is **archived, not cascaded** — the reversal of `DoseEventDao.kt`'s recorded cascade, argued here; and the as-needed ceiling **warns with a confirm** at the limit, never refuses. | ✅ **Resolved.** **Piece 1** (`e24e289`) — the data layer, **DB v7 → v8** (`MigrationFrom7To8Test`): `dose_events.notes` and a `DELETED` status excluded from every read that feeds adherence, History, the exports, the projection and the resolved anchor — the slot stays *claimed*, so a deleted dose is never re-materialised by the alarm path (the point of soft over hard); `reminders.instructions` and `dosesRemaining` (MT-07's course: counts down on Taken only, floored at zero, and `SchedulingEngine` returns no next occurrence at zero — the end condition beside `activeUntil`, as MT finding 3 asked); `medicines.archived` with `prnMaxDoses`/`prnWindowHours`; a `medicine_notes` table cascading with its medicine. `editDoseAmount` on a Taken dose now moves stock by the difference and re-writes the before/after pair (F.3 PT-F1 — R61 fixed the Missed→Taken order, this fixes the edit-afterwards); `reopen` gives stock back, clears the pair and re-arms through the coordinator; `reschedule` moves a pending slot and asks for a one-off alarm there (`onDoseRescheduled`, the snooze slot re-used); `archive` pauses the reminders and cancels their alarms rather than deleting them — a test caught that deleting them cascades to `dose_events`, which is the whole cascade the owner chose to keep history from. Eight JVM cases over a real in-memory Room. **Piece 2** (`edb6ff1`): `MissedDoseBackfiller` (DS-01, Dosage's `addMissedItems`) projects every history-independent reminder (FixedTime, ON_REMINDED intervals, windows — an ON_RESOLVED chain and a LinkedFollowing child depend on answers nobody gave, so they get no invented slots) from the last instant the app is known to have run (`app_last_seen_epoch_millis`, stamped on launch, boot and every alarm fire) to a minute ago, capped at **60 days**, through the same idempotent materialisation the alarm uses, so a slot the alarm did reach is never doubled; nothing is notified from here — R64's reposter keeps its own day-long window. Instructions go on the notification body before the stock count (MT-06), with `BigTextStyle` so three parts are readable. Three cases. **Piece 3** (`ffa8fec`) — the UI: the dose dialog gains a note, *Return to due* (re-posts the notification; medTimer RERAISE), *Move* (day + time; RESCHEDULE) and a two-tap soft *Delete* — the test that failed was the delete button below the fold of a phone-height dialog, which Robolectric's clipped touch injection refuses exactly as a thumb would; the reminder form gains the instructions line and "a set number of doses"; the medicine form the as-needed limit; the detail screen a notes journal (MT-04: three newest, show-all, edit and delete from the entry). Today lists as-needed medicines — those with no dosing reminder switched on — as standing rows with the platform's own relative time since the last dose and a Log chip (DS-10, PT-02/03); a projected slot can be taken early ("Take now", the row itself stays a non-button); the reminder's instructions sit on the row. The Log dose dialog backdates to any day and time (HM-26/MT-13), shows headroom against the ceiling as "N of M in the last H h · room again at T" (`prnHeadroom`, pure: room returns when the (taken−max+1)-th oldest dose leaves the window) and at the ceiling asks once and logs anyway on a yes. Settings › Data lists archived medicines with Restore and *Delete for good* — the only place the cascade is still reachable, behind a confirm that says the history goes too. The History export asks for a range preset (30 / 90 days, this year, everything) and an optional medicine before the file picker and fetches that scope once (`getInRangeOnce`) rather than exporting the visible window (MK-03/DS-F2); the CSV gains a `Notes` column, quoted like the name — a column change on the R61 shape. **Not done, by design:** the early-take window (MT-09) — "Take now" on any projected slot today covers the case without a per-reminder minutes field; amount-before-confirm on Today (DS-11) — the dialog already edits the amount after; DS-02's day-end auto-confirm, declined. **Left open:** the as-needed row's "last dose" reads any non-refill row, so an ad-hoc Skipped counts as a dose; the headroom counts Taken by `resolvedAt`, so a backdated log lands where it was taken, which is right, but a dose Taken with no `resolvedAt` (none exist after v3) would not count. Owed a device look: the notes journal on a small screen, the export dialog's chip row. Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **287 tests / 0 failures** (259 → 287), `lintDebug` green after one `PluralsCandidate` reword (fixed, not baselined, per CLAUDE.md); device suite **6/0/0** on LE2123 over the v8 migration, debug build reinstalled |  |
| R66 | **§9 #27 medicine cabinet — Appendix F.5 group 4, built to seven answers asked first:** the dosage form is the **icon picker** (each option carries a default unit, no column) over a separate catalogue; **tags become the kits** (group-by-tag in the sort menu, a persisted tag filter with a badge) over a new Kit entity; expiry is **month/year by default with an opened-date and shelf life** whose earlier date is the one that counts; **strength and Rx number** are the two fields adopted from HM-14/DT-10 (alias, composition, recommendations declined); search is a **live in-memory filter**, not FTS; low stock gets **the tab badge and a summary notification** — the one place the owner chose the larger option; grouping lives in the sort menu and the filter survives a restart. | ✅ **Resolved.** **Piece 1** (`35877a7`) — **DB v8 → v9** (`MIGRATION_8_9`, schema 9 exported, `MigrationFrom8To9Test`): `medicines.strength`, `rxNumber`, `openedDateEpochDay`, `useWithinDaysAfterOpening`, all nullable, the last two on the embedded `StockInfo`. `StockInfo.effectiveExpirationEpochDay` — a computed property, not a column, so neither Room nor the backup format carries a derived value — is the earlier of the printed expiry and opened + shelf life, and the four reads that used to touch `expirationDateEpochDay` (the engine's alert arming, the receiver's still-due check, the card's expired state, the expiry sort) all moved to it: HM-10 stored an opened date and did nothing with it; here eye drops opened in March expire in April whatever the box says, and the engine test pins that the alert arms on that date. `MedicineIconOption.defaultUnit` (tablet → tablet, drops → drop, inhaler → puff, …; the descriptive forms carry none) fills a blank unit field and **never overwrites a typed one** (`unitAfterPickingForm`, pure) — HM-05's `DrugType.doseType` re-thought without a schema change. `MonthYearField`: a month column and a year column, one radio node per option, storing the month's **last day** so "expired" flips on the first of the month after, which is what "EXP 03/2027" means; the schema keeps day granularity, an "Exact day" switch keeps the old picker, and a stored non-month-end day displays as a full date so switching modes never rounds a chosen date; leap February tested. Strength under the name on the card and the detail, and between name and form on the emergency card ("Sertraline 50 mg (tablets)" — still no dose amount, the exporter's rule); Rx in a `SelectionContainer` on the detail. Two lint rounds: `NonObservableLocale` on the month names — `LocalConfiguration.current.locales[0]`, bare, as `HistoryCalendarView` already does; even a `?: Locale.getDefault()` fallback trips it. **Piece 2** (`0f6a801`): `MedicinesSort.GROUP_BY_TAG` — `groupByTag` (pure) makes one sticky-headed section per tag in collation order, a two-tag medicine **under both** (a kit is where a medicine can be found, not the one place it is — which is why the grouped list keys on `tag/id` and hides the drag handle: an item that appears twice has no single index to move), the untagged bucket last, manual order inside. `filterEntries` (pure): the query matches case-insensitively in name, strength, indication or a tag name; selected tags pass a medicine carrying **any** of them; blank passes all. The filter is persisted **by tag name** (tags are keyed by name on the form; a renamed or deleted tag simply stops matching) beside the sort key and hide-empty; the search is not persisted — a search is a moment, a filter is a view. `MedicinesFilterBar` is its own composable: the field carries "Search medicines" as its content description, one node per chip, the badge count as a plural. An emptied list says "Nothing matches", not "No medicines". **Piece 3** (`021e51d`): `stockAttentionCount` (pure) — tracked and empty, tracked and at or below an *enabled* low-stock threshold, or expired by the effective date, counted per medicine not per reason — the cards' own predicates, so the number on the tab can never disagree with the cards behind it; `BadgedBox` on the Medicines `NavigationBarItem`, hidden at zero, its content description a plural. Every stock alert joins `STOCK_GROUP_KEY` with a delete intent; from two showing, `updateStockSummary()` posts a silent `InboxStyle` summary naming them, rebuilt after every post, every refill and every swipe, gone below two — R64's `updateGroupSummary` shape on the alerts' negative ids; the per-medicine cards stay because they carry Refill (Appendix F called them the better data). **Not done, by design:** HM-04 photos and MT-02 versioning (L, parked as F.5 says); alias/composition/recommendations (declined); per-tag user ordering (sections sort by name); FTS (declined; the cabinet is tens of rows). **Left open:** the summary's count is only as fresh as the last event — a notification the OS drops without a delete broadcast leaves it one high until the next post; the month picker's year range is fixed at last year to ten ahead. Owed a device look: the month/year picker feel, sticky headers on the six palettes, the badge in dark mode, plus the R59/R63/R65 list. On 13+ it is `LocaleManager.setApplicationLocales`, which the framework persists and applies process-wide (activities recreate; receivers, notification builders and Glance already speak the language), and `generateLocaleConfig` with `resources.properties` (`unqualifiedResLocale=en-US`, F-31) registers the shipped languages so Android's per-app language page lists exactly them — verified in the merged manifest, not added by hand. EmivitaApplication` holds exactly as on the phone) with two members swapped — no native library, a container over unencrypted in-memory Room — and the receivers are reached the way AlarmManager and the shade reach them, by the armed PendingIntent's own saved intent, given a real `PendingResult` so `goAsync()` is honoured and its `finish()` (the `finally` block) is the completion signal the test waits on. Idempotent on the phone (same engine, same answer), pure waste plus watchdog churn, and in the harness a race that failed two tests one run in three. Fixed at the flow, where the intent lives; `AlarmClockSettingFlowTest` pins it and was falsified against the old code (`[false, false]` — the re-emission). **Piece 2** (`925f3be`): `MedTimerSimulatorParityTest`, the eleven cases on `SchedulingEngine.occurrencesFor` and `OccurrenceProjectionCache` with the parity tests' calendar. Six parities: `standard` (the merged projection interleaves two medicines day by day, untracked stock projects no run-out), `interval` (10 h crossing midnight), `intervalStartsFromProcessed` (Emivita's `assumeOutstandingResolved` anchors on the outstanding dose's due time, which is medTimer's reminded time — the same three slots), `windowedInterval`, `dailyReminders` (its `intervalStart` has no Emivita counterpart; the window is the anchor), `noReminders`. | R71 | **§9 #32 database recovery — Lunar's `onCorruption` finding checked here; a different hole found and closed.** Lunar (`20271a2`) found that its `openOrRecover` never saw a corrupt file because Room's default callback inherits the framework's `DefaultDatabaseErrorHandler`, which deletes the file and reopens empty before any probe. Emivita's `EmivitaDatabase.build` was plain `Room.databaseBuilder(...).openHelperFactory(SupportOpenHelperFactory(passphrase))` — no probe, no set-aside, no recovery of any kind, and no spec paragraph claiming one. Read from `sqlcipher-android` 4.16.0's bytecode (`javap`): SQLCipher's `SupportHelper` passes `null` as the `DatabaseErrorHandler` and never calls Room's `Callback.onCorruption`, so Lunar's callback wrapper would be dead code here; and SQLCipher's own `DefaultDatabaseErrorHandler.onCorruption` returns without deleting when `hasCodec()` (a wrong passphrase looks like corruption, so it refuses to destroy the file). Net: on a corrupt `emivita.db` the file survived and the app **crashed at the first DAO call on every launch, with no route back**. | ✅ **Resolved** (one commit, code and record together, as asked), to two answers asked first: set-aside files beside the database, **kept forever, listed under Data & Backup** with one delete behind the lock; a **blocking dialog on launch, before the lock**, Restore from backup / Continue empty. `core/data/db/DatabaseRecovery.kt`: `openOrRecover` (Lunar's, the owner's own code — build, probe, close, set aside, rebuild, re-probe; a second failure rethrows), `DatabaseFiles.setAside` renaming the file and `-journal`/`-wal`/`-shm` under one stamp, `setAsideIncidents`/`deleteSetAsideFiles`, and `RecoveryNotice` — an on-disk marker, not a process flag, because the person may kill the app before answering and the next launch would open the empty database cleanly and forget to say anything. `EmivitaDatabase.open(context, name, factory)` takes the name and factory so the JVM test runs the production path on the framework factory (SQLCipher cannot load there) and the device test on the real SQLCipher factory with a throwaway name — the owner's file is never touched. **No production wrapper**, deliberately: `DatabaseRecoveryDeviceTest` on the LE2123 wrote garbage to `androidTest-recovery.db`, opened through the real factory and passphrase, and got `recovered == true`, the set-aside byte-for-byte equal, an empty count, and a fresh ciphertext file — the bytecode reading checked, not trusted. `DatabaseFileTest` pins Lunar's finding as a fact about the framework factory (garbage → `recovered == false`, no set-aside, bytes gone) and runs the recovery with Lunar's `KeepFileOnCorruptionFactory` kept test-side; a stale journal cannot be shown moving through an open, because SQLite discards an invalid hot journal itself — the rename is tested as the file operation it is. `DatabaseRecoveredDialog` before `AppLockGate`, gated on the marker read once the profile list has arrived (that read is what opened the database); Restore pushes Data & Backup onto the unchanged root once. `SetAsideFilesSection` under Delete everything, absent with no files, `incidents` injectable for its test. Strings in both languages. Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **383 tests / 0 failures** (372 → 383), `lintDebug` green; device suite **7/0/0** (6 → 7); debug build installed. **Owed on the device:** the dialog itself on a corrupt file — the device test proves the path, not the screen, and provoking it on the owner's phone means corrupting the owner's file. Parking lot: salvaging rows from a set-aside file (same Keystore passphrase; a future "try to open it read-only") |  |

A-24: a linked follow-up was absent from the projection until its parent had actually fired, so Today, the widget and the stock run-out did not see it. A-41: the compute budget was checked once per medicine, so one pathological reminder dragged its medicine's others past it. A-45: the projection was published once at the end of a pass. | ✅ **Resolved.** Answers: **the day is the calendar day, 00:00–23:59** — an overnight slot at 02:00 belongs to the day it falls on, like a FixedTime at 02:00; and the follow-up is shown **as an estimate**, "≈ 09:00 · Estimated: 60 min after the 08:00 dose". **Piece 1** (`07786b9`): `windowSlotOnDay` adds a day to an end strictly before the start (medTimer's rule — equal start and end stays a one-slot window); the day loop starts a day back for an overnight window, since yesterday's may still be running; `DailyWindow.isOvernight`; the form says "Ends the next day at 06:00" instead of leaving the person to guess (`WindowEndsNextDayNote`, tested), and Save was never disabled for it. The R70 pins flip — with one fact worth its own sentence: asked at day 1 00:00, Emivita answers a slot *that night* (00:01 / 02:40) that medTimer does not, because medTimer's reminder carries `intervalStart` and did not exist the evening before, while a DailyWindow has no start anchor and fires the remaining slots of a window it finds itself inside — **A-06's documented rule, not an overnight defect**; the chain after it is parity, and both parity files say so. **Piece 2** (`788e1be`): `ProjectedOccurrence.estimated` + `afterEpochMillis`; the cache runs two passes per medicine — the reminders that compute their own slots, then the linked children: their one real slot anchored to the parent's most recent real event (unchanged), plus an estimate after every projected parent slot beyond it; ON_RESOLVED children estimate from the parent's slot too, the resolution time being unknown, which is what the "≈" is for. Parents are same-medicine (the form offers nothing else); one level (A-23). Today's node time takes "≈" when every entry at that instant is an estimate and the row says what it follows; the widget's time takes the "≈"; the medicines list only sorts by the next slot and shows nothing. Stock run-out counts the follow-ups: `linkedAndAmount` moves from 1970-01-12 to 1970-01-04, medTimer's 12 → 11 → 9 → 8 → 6. "Take now" stays available on an estimate. **Piece 3** (`3a6d398`): the budget check before every reminder's walk, and a trip keeps the reminders already walked — the first cut lost them by breaking out of the medicine loop before the medicine's slots were added, and the new test caught it with its own clock readings in the message; a partial answer, not a lost one. The state is published after each medicine with `ProjectionState.inProgress = true` and once complete without it (medTimer publishes at each day boundary); consumers reading an absence wait for the complete state, the rule `truncated` already set. **Found on the way, a real one:** the receiver harness (R70) leaked each test's container — the projection loop and the settings collectors kept running on a connection Robolectric had torn down, which in native SQLite mode is a JVM crash (`EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION` in `libandroid_runtime.dll` on `arch_disk_io_0`), not an exception; it had passed twice by ordering luck. `AppContainer.shutdown()` (test-only, `@VisibleForTesting`) cancels the scope and closes the database; the harness calls it from every `@After`. Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **395 tests / 0 failures** (383 → 395), `lintDebug` green; device suite **7/0/0**; debug build installed. **Owed on the device:** an overnight window's slots on Today across midnight; an estimated follow-up row and the widget's "≈" |  |

`publish_results` forced off on a private repository, the `repo_token` PAT line present and commented with where to get one) — both state in their header that the upload to code scanning **fails on a private repository without GitHub Advanced Security**, which is the expected result until the repository is public or GHAS is on, and the files are kept so that day needs no writing. `ci.yml` is two jobs with no dependency: `verify` (the §10 triad in *one* Gradle invocation with `--continue`, so a failing test still lints — R23's reports) and `release-gate` (`assembleRelease` + `tools/r8_check.py`, the APK as an artifact); the old one-job rationale was about not splitting the triad, which is still not split. `dependency-review.yml` not written: it fires only on pull requests and the ledger shows work landing on master directly — the one to add with the first PR. **The gate, `tools/workflow_check.py`:** every `uses:` pinned to a 40-char commit SHA with a `# vX.Y.Z` comment (all eight actions across the three files, resolved live from the tags rather than copied from medTimer — annotated tag objects dereferenced to the commit, `gradle/actions` and `codeql-action` being two); top-level `permissions:` present; with `--resolve`, each comment's tag must dereference to exactly that SHA on GitHub, and **every `with:` key must be an input of the action's own `action.yml` at that tag**; and actionlint 1.7.12 (a binary under `tools/bin/`, gitignored). **Falsified three ways** before the commit: an unpinned `@v4` → FAIL naming the line; a SHA off by one hex digit → FAIL naming the action and the SHA GitHub has; a misspelt input `java-versoin` → **actionlint let it through** — it validates inputs only for actions it knows by version tag, never for a SHA pin, which is exactly the pin this repository requires — so the input check was written into the gate itself, and the third falsification then failed as it should, listing the action's twenty declared inputs. Verified: triad `EXIT=0`, **407 / 0** (no app code changed), `lintDebug` green; `python tools/workflow_check.py --resolve` PASS (3 workflows, every pin resolved, every input declared, actionlint clean). The push is the only "run", and it dies in three seconds on the account, not on these files. **Left open:** F-21 by name; making the repository public or buying GHAS is the owner's call and outside the code | R86 |

| R80 | **R75 follow-up 1 — the Alarm tier rang once when the phone was unlocked; it rings until answered now.** Structural, not a bug in the loop: the loop lived in `AlarmActivity`, which Android launches from the full-screen intent only when the phone is locked or the display is off; unlocked, the system shows a heads-up instead, whose channel sound plays once. It stops when the dose is answered anywhere (every dismissal now goes through `ReminderNotifications.dismiss`, which cancels and stops — five call sites cancelled directly before, and a foreground service's notification cannot be cancelled from outside it), and it polls the dose every 5 s so a path that does not cannot leave it ringing; the swipe action dismisses too. Started from the alarm-fired receiver: Android 12+ allows that (an exact alarm firing is on the exemption list — the phone logged `Background started FGS: Allowed … ALARM_MANAGER_WHILE_IDLE`); a device that refuses gets the notification with `FLAG_INSISTENT`, which loops the channel's tone. `FOREGROUND_SERVICE` and `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PLAYBACK` enter the manifest, whose comment had said no code wanted the former; it says why now. **Verified on the phone, unlocked, on the debug build with a seeded alarm:** fired 09:28:00, the alarm-stream player `state:started` at every 10 s sample for seventy seconds, the heads-up showing Prendi / Salta; Prendi from the heads-up → no player and no service within three seconds. `AlarmRingServiceTest` (four cases under the receiver harness): the post hands the notification to the service and a dismissal sends it a stop; the service runs foreground under the reminder's id and stops itself once the dose is answered elsewhere; unanswered for ten minutes it stops with the notification detached, not removed; a stop for another dose is ignored. The service measures its timeout with `elapsedRealtime`, which is also what Robolectric advances. Triad `EXIT=0`, **431 / 0**, lint clean, R8 gate PASS, merged release manifest carries exactly the two new permissions. R75's follow-ups are both closed |  |

The English section headers now sit before the same keys in the Italian file so the two read in parallel; the duplicated Stage A header at `values/strings.xml:191` is gone. `TranslationCompletenessTest` now requires the `identical` list to be exact both ways (mutation-checked) and matches every Formatter conversion. **Preferences, not errors, for the owner** (correttore's 🟡 perimeter): *Cancella la ricerca* vs the R75 *Azzera* (Android and medTimer both say *Cancella* for clear); *Impaginazione di Oggi* vs *Disposizione*; the launcher icon's `#EFEFEF` tile printing at `#F1F1F2` on the `#F5F6F8` splash (measured); and — checked against the reference apps as the owner asked — *Rinvia* vs *Posticipa* for snooze: medTimer's community Italian and Google's Clock both use *Posticipa*, R67 chose *rinvia* deliberately. **Device check 2, auto-start, was a defect:** the LE2123 on Android 14 has neither `com.coloros.safecenter` nor `com.oneplus.security`; its startup list is `com.oplus.battery/com.oplus.startupapp.view.StartupAppListActivity`, so none of the 14 components resolved and the button was never drawn. Added to the list and to `<queries>` — and then found *exported but guarded* by `oplus.permission.OPLUS_COMPONENT_SAFE` (`am start` from shell: `SecurityException`; every other startup activity in that package the same), so `intentFor` now also requires that the resolved activity's `permission` be null or held: a button that throws is worse than no button, and on this phone there is correctly no button (`AutoStartSettingsTest`: found when open, skipped when guarded). | R82 | **A cleanup pass that became an audit: five review passes over the whole tree, the mechanical half made computable, and the first five fixes.** The owner asked for overbloated code, unnecessary comments, "AI slop", gaps and bugs removed, with the review and optimizer skills, and for modernization to be assessed. The bloat premise turned out to be half wrong and the gap premise badly understated. **Not slop:** zero `TODO`/`FIXME`/`XXX`/`HACK` in 119 files, and the 23% comment density (4,782 of 20,675 main lines) is mandated by §10 — the rationale comments *are* this ledger's working copy. The owner's ruling was therefore "only wrong or redundant ones, and cut the rest to 2-3 sentences max", which made comment *length* a computable rule rather than a matter of taste. **Tools (in `tools/`, commands in CLAUDE.md):** `code_health.py --check` exits 1 on comment blocks over three sentences, imports no longer used, and top-level declarations referenced only where they are declared; `--report` adds the rows needing judgement (duplicate blocks, oversized files); `--graph` emits one file's symbol-reference graph; `--selftest` proves each detector can still fire. Its first three runs were wrong in three different ways, each worth recording because each is a general trap: a `${...}` template span is live Kotlin, so blanking it with the literal made `EXPORT_FILE_STAMP` — used only inside an interpolated file name — look dead; a quote nested inside a template (`"${"%02d".format(h)}"`) mis-paired the scanner, blanked a real `*/`, and ran a three-line comment 296 lines to end of file, which is why the lexer now knows comments and literals in one pass; and 54 of 86 "unused" imports were `getValue`/`setValue`, which Kotlin resolves by *convention* for every `var x by remember { ... }` and never writes out, so `CONVENTION_IMPORTS` is exempt and a textual census must never judge them. The verification that "confirmed" those 86 used the same textual evidence as the finding, which is no verification at all; the compiler is the verifier, and it passed on the 29 the tool kept. **The optimizer, honestly:** both oversized files are a single connected component, so the structure does not collapse the partition problem and a search was warranted rather than assumed (`split_plan.py --explain/--greedy/--solve` scores a split by boundary-crossing references, `private` symbols needing widening to `internal`, lines over a 450-line cap, part count and size spread). `meta-optimizer` (engine self-test 78 passed, spec fully computable, 0 model turns) **lost to a greedy baseline on one file** — 336 against 161 on `SettingsDetailScreens` — because part labels are interchangeable and a population search has no gradient across symmetric plateaus; partition problems want local search. Adding move-and-swap refinement over every seed settled it: `SettingsDetailScreens` 538 → **136** (3 parts, 309/324/315 lines, 4 crossing references, **zero** visibility widenings) from the greedy-k3 seed, and `AddReminderScreen` 723 → **396.7** (3 parts, 361/323/449, 22 crossings, 7 widenings) from the *engine's* seed, which beat the greedy-seeded 427.7. So the engine earned its keep as a seed generator on the hub-shaped file and was wasted effort on the clean-splitting one. Both splits are computed and **not applied** — a 1,300-line diff in the same build as five behaviour fixes makes a regression unattributable. **Five review passes** (`dev-engineering-suite:android-auditor` plus four briefed agents over scheduling/alarms, the data layer, the Compose UI, and the test suite itself) returned ~60 findings, of which the ledger-worthy are: a **recovery overwrites the only off-device backup with an empty one** (`AutoSyncManager` had zero references to `RecoveryNotice`, and its swap did `delete()` then an unchecked `renameTo`, so a provider without `renameDocument` leaves the folder holding only `.new`, which nothing reads, while the screen looks healthy); a **"password-protected" export silently writing plaintext** (`pendingExportPassword` is a plain `remember` across the SAF activity, so a recreated activity wrote an unencrypted zip of the whole history and reported "Backup exported"); a **lost Keystore wrap key being an unrecoverable crash loop** that `openOrRecover` structurally cannot see, because `sqlcipherFactory(...)` is evaluated as an *argument* to `open(...)` and so runs outside the retry — found independently by two passes; `setAlarmClock` called without the `canScheduleExact()` gate its own KDoc says protects it — also found twice independently; medicine names reaching logcat in release with no `-assumenosideeffects`; and the privacy setting that masks names in every notification not reaching `AlarmActivity`, the one screen that shows over the lock screen. The test-suite pass found the sharpest thing in the batch: **the R81 gate was the weakest of the three sibling gates.** `LiveControlBesideLabelGateTest` had no lower bound on files scanned, so renaming `feature/` would turn it green over an unscanned tree where `HardcodedUiTextGateTest:107` asserts `sources.size > 50`; it checked `onCheckedChange = null` but never the `toggleable` row CLAUDE.md names as the other half, so deleting a row's modifier left an inert control and a passing build; it ignored `RadioButton` at 18 sites; and `args.contains("contentDescription")` was satisfied by `contentDescription = null`. All four closed, and widening it immediately failed twice in ways worth keeping: the negative lookahead had to move from `=\s*(?!null)` to `=(?!\s*null)` because `\s*` is free to match nothing and let `" null"` through, and `MedicinesScreen.kt:200` is *correct* code in a third shape — `RadioButton(onClick = null)` as a `DropdownMenuItem`'s `leadingIcon`, where the item's own `onClick` is the single node — so the rule was widened rather than given an exception. Five fixes landed: the auto-sync recovery guard, the kept `.prev` generation with both `renameTo` results checked, the export that refuses to downgrade (`backup_export_password_lost`, EN + IT), the widened gate, and 29 unused imports across 21 files. **One defect class, five instances, worth more than the instances:** `renameTo`'s discarded boolean, `openOutputStream`'s null, `resolveActivity`'s unchecked `permission`, `pendingExportPassword` going null, and `DocumentFile.renameTo` in the set-aside path — each a failure that returns `false` or `null` where nothing looked (§7.1 rule 4's shape, and rule 17's). **Modernization, assessed only, as asked:** AGP 8.13.2 / Kotlin 2.1.0 / compileSdk 36 are pinned with the reason written in `build.gradle.kts`, and `app/build.gradle.kts:264` records that compileSdk 37 needs AGP 9.x, which drops the `org.jetbrains.kotlin.android` plugin and forces a DSL and module-split migration. Mimirae and Lunar both moved to AGP 9.4 / Kotlin 2.4 / targetSdk 37 on 2026-10-11, so the pin is now a divergence from the siblings rather than the house default — but it remains a correct decision for a single-module app, and reversing it is a pass of its own, not a side effect of a cleanup. Robolectric stays at 4.14.1: 4.17 broke Room tests in Mimirae, so that bump gets verified, never assumed. | ✅ **Partly resolved; the rest is itemised, not forgotten.** Shipped: the five fixes above, both tools with a 14-case self-test, and the ledger-worthy findings recorded here. **Unverified by test and labelled as such (§7.1 rule 5):** the two `AutoSyncManager` guards — its write path is a `Channel` plus a 12 s `delay` launched on `Dispatchers.IO`, which virtual time cannot advance, so covering them needs a seam and that is a change in its own right. **Open for the owner, each a behaviour or wording decision rather than a gap:** `rememberSaveable` appears **twice** in 119 main files, so every form in the app loses its input on rotation (and `AppLockGate` re-challenges biometrics); drag-to-reorder on Medicines cancels itself the moment it works, because `pointerInput(index)` is keyed on the index the drag mutates, so only the TalkBack move actions function; a LinkedFollowing child is cancelled outright with no catch-up whenever its parent's alarm is delivered later than `delayMinutes`; `AlarmRingService` calls `startForeground` then unconditionally detaches, so the Alarm tier may ring from a background service — and `AlarmRingServiceTest:135` asserts `lastForegroundNotificationId`, a proxy Robolectric leaves set after `stopForeground`, so the test passes while the property is false; a snoozed dose is re-posted on the next launch, which for an Alarm-tier medicine means a full ring nobody asked for; and the Keystore-loss path, where the honest fix is not merely moving the factory inside `build` — a file set aside after the wrap key is gone can never be decrypted, so the recovery wording that promises recoverability would become false and needs the owner's words. Also held: the 172 over-cap comment blocks (2,456 lines) and the two computed splits. **Verified:** triad `EXIT=0`, **438 / 0 / 0 skipped** on fresh XML (the count is unchanged because the new assertions live inside `theGateItselfCanFail`, not in new test methods), lint clean, `code_health.py --selftest` 14/14, `--check` 0 unused imports and 0 dead declarations, `split_plan.py` scores reproducible from the committed graphs. The gate's first run **failed** on two real defects of its own before passing, which is the only reason its four closed holes can be claimed. Not run this pass: the release build and R8 gate, and anything on the device — the §9 #34 sitting is still owed and no night alarms fire on the owner's phone. |  |

| R103 | **Appendix E's C F-3, open since R61: a dose skipped against short stock and later taken spent too little.** Skipping records the pair the dose *would* have used, floored at zero by `hypotheticalStockSentinels`; `switchStatus` to Taken then spent that floored difference whatever the stock had become. With 1 left and a 2-unit dose, a skip recorded (1, 0); after a refill to 30, the switch spent 1 and the bottle read 29 instead of 28, for good. The 2026-10-20 medTimer census found it still open. A skipped dose consumed nothing, so taking it now consumes from today's stock: the switch to Taken recomputes the pair against the current quantity, rewrites it, and decrements by it (relative, R27); the switch to Skipped still hands back the recorded pair, so switching stays exactly reversible. | ✅ **Resolved.** `DoseEventRepositoryTest`: *a dose skipped against short stock and taken after a refill spends the whole dose* failed before the change (`expected:<28.0> but was:<29.0>`) and passes after it; the existing reversibility and short-stock tests still pass. |  |
