#### 2. Daily Pill (v1.3.3, versionCode 16, minSdk 16 / target 30; Kotlin, no DB; last release 2021)

Root `C:/Users/User/Downloads/xapps/DailyPill-master/app/src/main/java/br/com/gualandi/dailypill/` (abbreviated `dp/`).

**Why it is so small.** One medicine, one daily time, and the entire state is five `SharedPreferences` keys (`dp/DataModel.kt:31-36`: `drug_taken_timestamp`, `reminder_enabled`, `reminder_time`, `reminder_lock_screen`, `next_alarm_timestamp`). "Taken today" is `timestamp ≥ local midnight` (`:69-76`); `takeDrugNow()` overwrites the single timestamp (`:56-61`), so there is no history at all — only the last press survives. The 1.3.3 changelog says the weekday was added "in preparation for the planned multi-medication update" (`CHANGELOG.txt`), which never shipped. The product page (`docs/index.md:9-15`) states the intent: remember *when* you took it "if you need to wait a certain amount of time before eating", a reminder that "will always be there once you wake up" whatever the hour, deliberately unobtrusive ("doesn't set off any noisy alarms"), answerable "without unlocking your phone or entering your PIN".

**The lock-screen mechanism, exactly.** `AlarmReceiver` (`dp/AlarmReceiver.kt:27-33`) re-arms tomorrow and calls `sendReminderNotification(now, shouldInterrupt = true)`. That builds a notification on a channel created at IMPORTANCE_HIGH (`dp/Notifications.kt:55-58`), `PRIORITY_HIGH`, `CATEGORY_REMINDER`, whose content intent is `ReminderActivity` (`:77-81, 90`), and — only when the preference `reminder_lock_screen` is on (default true, `dp/DataModel.kt:113-115`) and this is the alarm firing rather than a re-post — `setFullScreenIntent(pendingIntent, true)` (`:97-99`). Manifest declares `USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT` (`AndroidManifest.xml:6`). `ReminderActivity` is a transparent-theme, `excludeFromRecents`, empty-affinity activity (manifest `:24-28`) that calls `setShowWhenLocked(true)` on API ≥ 27 (else `FLAG_SHOW_WHEN_LOCKED`) (`dp/ReminderActivity.kt:34-40`) and shows an `AlertDialog` containing a single 175 dp-tall "Yes, I have" button (`res/layout/reminder_dialog.xml`) and a "Not yet" negative button (`:52-58`). "Yes" records the timestamp once per day and cancels the notification (`:64-69`); "Not yet" just dismisses the dialog — no snooze alarm is set, the notification remains as the standing reminder. There is **no keyguard dismissal, no `turnScreenOn`, no sound, no vibration** beyond what the HIGH channel does by default; the same content intent means a lock-screen tap on the notification also lands in the show-when-locked dialog. So: notification + full-screen intent + show-when-locked activity + one button — not a widget, not a notification action.

**Other mechanics.** `setWhen(reminderTime)` and `setTimeoutAfter(midnight − now)` (`dp/Notifications.kt:91-92`) make the notification expire itself at end of day; boot/locale/time/zone changes re-post a *silent* copy (`setOnlyAlertOnce`, `:94-96`) if today's dose is overdue (`possiblyAddMissedNotification` `:126-131`; `dp/AlarmInitReceiver.kt:36-48` handles `BOOT_COMPLETED`, `LOCALE_CHANGED`, `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED`, `TIME_CHANGED`, `TIMEZONE_CHANGED`). Alarms use `setExact` (`:162-172`) with the planned instant stored (`:174-176`); `MainActivity.checkForMissedAlarms()` (`dp/MainActivity.kt:90-114`) compares it on resume and, if the alarm is > 1 min overdue and uptime > 5 min, shows "Missed reminders — this might have been caused by your device's battery saving settings" and re-arms. A "Forget last time?" menu action undoes the press (`:120-130`). Settings has a "test reminder" preference that posts the real notification with `shouldInterrupt = true` (`dp/SettingsActivity.kt:50, 70`).

##### 2.2 Behaviour cases worth pinning (if adopted)

1. **Quiet lock-screen card (DP-01/DP-03).** Given medicine X is HIGH tier with "show quietly on lock screen" on and the device is locked; when X's reminder fires; then a show-when-locked card with Take / Skip / Snooze appears with no sound and no vibration; pressing Take resolves the dose (stock decremented, nag cancelled) without the keyguard being dismissed; given `canUseFullScreenIntent()` is false, the card is not shown and the shade notification carries a "Show" action instead.
2. **Reminder self-expiry (DP-02).** Given a Daily 08:00 STANDARD reminder is unanswered; when the next 08:00 occurrence is posted; then the previous day's notification is gone and Today still lists yesterday's row as missed; given the medicine is locked-until-taken, the previous notification stays.
3. **Missed-alarm detection (DP-04).** Given the earliest armed alarm was for T and the app is opened at T + 2 min with no DoseEvent materialised for that slot and uptime ≥ 5 min; then Notifications & Alerts shows "a reminder due at T did not fire" and everything is re-armed. Given uptime < 5 min, or the app is opened at T + 30 s, nothing is shown. Given the slot *was* materialised (the alarm fired but the user did not answer), nothing is shown.
4. **Test reminder (DP-05).** Given the user taps "Send a test reminder" on the ALARM row; then a notification on `reminders_alarm_v2` appears with the same presentation path as a real one and no DoseEvent is written; it disappears within 30 s if untouched.

##### 2.3 Findings (Emivita defects this comparison exposed)

- **DP-F1 — A HIGH/STANDARD reminder is not answerable from a secure lock screen that hides content.** `ReminderNotificationBuilder.kt` sets no `setVisibility` and no `setPublicVersion`, so on devices whose lock screen redacts private notifications the shade shows "Content hidden" without the Take/Skip/Snooze actions; the only lock-screen path is the ALARM tier. Daily Pill sidesteps it with the show-when-locked activity; Pill Time with a public version (PT-06). Whether this counts as a defect depends on §11's still-open "hide names" decision — it is at least an undocumented limitation.
- **DP-F2 — Emivita cannot tell the user an armed alarm was suppressed** (see DP-04): `rescheduleOnLaunch()` repairs silently. The `BatteryOptimizationCard` copy says reminders *may* be delayed; the app has the data to say one *was*.
- (Same as DT-F1: no notification timeout / supersession.)

##### 2.4 Spot-check list

1. **DP-01** — that the lock-screen dialog needs no unlock: `dp/ReminderActivity.kt:34-40` sets only `showWhenLocked`; there is no `KeyguardManager.requestDismissKeyguard` anywhere (`grep -rn requestDismissKeyguard` → 0). Also confirm the content intent (`dp/Notifications.kt:80-81, 90`) is the *same* PendingIntent as the full-screen one, so a lock-screen tap reaches the same activity.
2. **DP-02** — `setTimeoutAfter` semantics on the target API: the timeout is measured from post time (`endOfDay − now`, `:92`); a re-post at 23:59 gets a 1-minute timeout. Verify Emivita's proposed "until next slot" variant handles a re-post after the slot passed (timeout ≤ 0 → do not set).
3. **DP-04 guards** — `dp/MainActivity.kt:98` uses `nowTimestamp − 60 000 < nextAlarmTimestamp → return`, i.e. one minute of AlarmManager slack; `:104` five minutes of uptime. Confirm `next_alarm_timestamp` is updated on *every* arm (`dp/Notifications.kt:176`) including the `onlyTomorrow` path from the receiver (`dp/AlarmReceiver.kt:29`), otherwise a stale value produces a false warning the day after a successful fire.

---

#### 3. Pill Time (v20.0, versionCode 20, minSdk 23 / target 34; Java, raw SQLiteOpenHelper; active, last changelog "fix for errors when saving meds (#31)")

Root `C:/Users/User/Downloads/xapps/PillTime-main/app/src/main/java/com/cliambrown/pilltime/` (abbreviated `pt/`).

**The PRN model.** A `Med` has `maxDose` and `doseHours` (`pt/meds/Med.java:27-28`), rendered as "max 4 / 24 h" or "max 2 / day" (`pt/utilities/Utils.java:194-205`; plurals `max_dose_per_hour`, `max_dose_per_day`, `res/values/strings.xml:7-14`); both must be ≥ 1 (`:94-97`). A `Dose` is `count` (a double — half tablets are allowed), `takenAt` (Unix seconds), `notify`, `notifySound` (`pt/doses/Dose.java:18-21`). Everything else is derived on read in `DbHelper.updateMedTimes()` (`pt/utilities/DbHelper.java:471-536`): `activeDoseCount = SUM(count)` over `[now − doseHours, now]` (`:473-475`, query `:451-456`); `latestDose` = newest with `takenAt ≤ now` (`:478-494` — a future-dated entry is ignored until its time comes); `nextExpiringDose` = the *oldest* dose still inside the window (`:501-516`); `pastDayDoseCount` over the last 86 400 s (`:523`); `currentInventory` = `reportedInventory − SUM(count since inventoryReportedAt)`, floored at 0 (`:524-534`). A dose is "active" while `takenAt ≤ now < takenAt + doseHours·3600` (`pt/doses/Dose.java:122-128`).

**What "max dosage" enforces: nothing.** `PillTimeApplication.addDose()` (`pt/PillTimeApplication.java:244-269`) inserts, schedules the expiry alarm, and broadcasts; there is no comparison with `maxDose`. The ceiling is purely a *display* state: the "N taken over the past H h" line turns the theme's red when `activeDoseCount ≥ maxDose` (`pt/meds/MedsRecycleViewAdapter.java:231-236`; `pt/meds/MedActivity.java:267-271`; `Utils.buildTakenInPastString` `:144-163` sets the big bold number in that colour). The long-press quick-add on the list's "+" button logs `defaultDoseCount` at now with no confirmation (`MedsRecycleViewAdapter.java:80-96`, toast "Dose saved").

**"Time since" and "when can I take the next one".** Each med card shows "last taken 2h 13m ago" (`:219-230`; `Utils.getRelativeTimeSpanString` `:67-124` builds two-unit strings such as "1d 3h", never "1d 0h", and drops to "1d 3m" when the middle unit is zero) and, whenever any dose is active, "**1** expire**s** in 3h 47m (at 6:12 PM)" from the oldest in-window dose (`:199-218`; `Utils.simpleFutureTime` `:49-65`; plural `expires` `strings.xml:52-55`). That line is the "next one" answer: when the oldest in-window dose expires, `activeDoseCount` drops by its count and headroom returns. It is shown regardless of whether the ceiling is reached, so it doubles as "your earliest dose stops counting at …". The detail screen adds an optional "N taken over the past day" line when `showDayDoseCount` is on and the window is not already 24 h (`MedActivity.java:273-280`). Both screens tick every 60 s via `java.util.Timer` → `notifyItemChanged(i, "update_times")` (`pt/MainActivity.java:190-199`, `MedActivity.java:286-295`).

**Expiration notifications.** `scheduleNotification()` (`pt/PillTimeApplication.java:271-289`) cancels and re-arms one alarm per dose at `takenAt + doseHours` with `AlarmManager.setWindow(RTC_WAKEUP, t, 10 min)` — the comment at `:285-287` says exact alarms were dropped because of "permission complexities"; no `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` in the manifest. The alarm is skipped when the dose's `notify` is false or the instant is already past. `AlarmBroadcastReceiver` (`pt/notifications/AlarmBroadcastReceiver.java:36-87`) builds a private notification "<Med> dose expired" (`PRIORITY_HIGH`, `VISIBILITY_PRIVATE`) with a **public version** "Pill Time: A dose expired" (`:51-60, 66-67`), `setSilent` per dose (`:60, 72`), `setAutoCancel(true)`, content intent → `MedActivity`; the channel is IMPORTANCE_DEFAULT (`:419`); `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` is checked at post time (`:74-84`) and requested from the dose editor when the notify switch is turned on (`pt/doses/EditDoseActivity.java:52-58`). Defaults cascade global prefs → per-med override → per-dose switches (`Med.java:421-431`; `root_preferences.xml` `notify_default`, `notify_sound_default`). `BootReceiver` re-arms only doses whose expiry is still in the future (`pt/notifications/BootReceiver.java:21-44`, `DbHelper.getActiveDoses()` `:538-562`); `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` is not handled, so an app update drops pending expiry alarms (Pill Time's own defect, consistent with its disclaimer).

**Inventory.** Tracked per med as a *reported* count at a *timestamp* (`Med.java:30-32`; editor shows "Remaining doses last recorded 3d ago", `pt/meds/EditMedActivity.java:161-171`, string `:111`); current stock is derived (above), shown as "12 / 30 left in stock" (`Med.getInventoryStr()` `:365-367`), with a warning icon when `currentInventory == 0` or `current/reported ≤ 0.2` (`INVENTORY_WARN_THRESHOLD` `:22`, `getIsInventoryLow()` `:222-229` — note the `&&`/`||` precedence there: the ratio branch is evaluated even when tracking is off; harmless because every caller checks `getIsInventoryTracked()` first, `MedsRecycleViewAdapter.java:257`).

**What it refuses, and how it says so.** The README states "not appropriate for meds that need to be taken regularly on a set schedule"; there is no fixed-time reminder anywhere — the only alarm in the app is the dose-expiry one. The two disclaimer paragraphs (`strings.xml:165-167`: no medical advice; "cannot be guaranteed to provide notifications at specific times… restarting the phone may disable scheduled notifications") are shown (a) on the main screen **only in the empty state** — they sit inside `ll_main_no_meds` (`res/layout/activity_main.xml:33-82`), toggled by `MainActivity.onUpdateMeds()` (`pt/MainActivity.java:174-181`); and (b) permanently as two read-only preferences under Settings › About (`res/xml/root_preferences.xml:87-102`). No acceptance gate, no dialog.

##### 3.2 Behaviour cases worth pinning (if adopted)

1. **Rolling-window ceiling (PT-01/PT-02).** Given medicine X has ceiling 4 per 24 h and TAKEN rows of 1 at now−23 h, 1 at now−6 h, 1 at now−1 h (one of them a *scheduled* dose); when the Log-dose dialog opens for X with amount 1; then it shows "3 of 4 in the last 24 h" with no warning; when the amount is changed to 2; then the warning "would be 5 of max 4" appears and the Log button stays enabled; when logged; then a 5th unit is recorded and X's card shows the attention state. Given the oldest row is edited to now−25 h; then the count is 2 and the attention state clears.
2. **Headroom instant (PT-03).** Given X is at 4 of 4 with rows at now−20 h (1), now−10 h (2), now−2 h (1); when the card renders; then "may take again in 4 h (at HH:MM)" is computed from the now−20 h row; given the ceiling is 3 and the same rows, the instant is the now−10 h row's expiry (the first row's exit leaves 3 ≥ 3). Given X is below the ceiling; then only "last taken 2 h ago" is shown.
3. **Window-closed alert (PT-05).** Given X has "tell me when I may take X again" on and a dose is logged at 14:00 with a 6 h window; then an inexact alarm is armed for 20:00 and, when it fires, a notification "You may take X again" (public version: "A dose window has closed") opens Today; given the dose's recorded time is edited to 15:00 before it fires, the alarm moves to 21:00; given the dose is switched to Skipped, the alarm is cancelled; given the flag is off, nothing is armed.
4. **Future-dated PRN dose (PT-09).** Given a PRN dose of X is logged with a time 2 h in the future; then X's "last taken" ignores it until that time passes and it does not count toward the ceiling until then.
5. **Recount from last count (PT-07).** Given X's stock was set to 30 at T0 and since then 3 TAKEN rows of 1 and one REFILLED row of 10 exist, one TAKEN row having later been switched to Skipped; when "Recount" runs; then quantity = 30 − 2 + 10 = 38 regardless of what the mutated column held; given no count timestamp exists (pre-migration medicine), Recount is unavailable rather than guessing.
6. **Disclaimer placement (PT-10).** Given Settings › About; then two non-interactive rows carry the two sentences; given the PRN ceiling form; then the "information, not advice" sentence is visible above the ceiling fields; no dialog is ever shown on launch.

##### 3.3 Findings (Emivita defects this comparison exposed)

- **PT-F1 — `editDoseAmount()` leaves stock inconsistent** (`em/core/data/repository/DoseEventRepository.kt:247`; the inventory's own row says "neither touches stock"). R61 fixed the Missed → Taken ordering, but a Taken dose whose amount is later edited from 1 to 2 still leaves stock decremented by 1. Pill Time's derived inventory (PT-07) cannot have this class of bug. A `recountStock` (PT-07 note) or a `stockAfter` correction in `editDoseAmount` closes it.
- **PT-F2 — PRN logging has no "you logged this N minutes ago" guard**: `logAdhocDose()` `:265-287` neither reads `getMostRecentForMedicine()` nor any window. With DITMM's slot guard and Pill Time's ceiling both absent, a user who forgets they logged 20 minutes ago gets no signal at all. This is the gap PT-01/DT-03 fill; recorded here as a defect because the app's stated purpose (adherence, single user with memory load) makes silence the wrong default.
- (Same as DP-F1: no public notification version → not answerable from a redacting lock screen.)

##### 3.4 Spot-check list

1. **PT-03 semantics** — that "expires" is the *oldest* in-window dose, not the latest: `pt/utilities/DbHelper.java:501-506` orders `takenAt ASC LIMIT 1` within `[earliestActiveTakenAt, now]`; and that it is shown whenever `activeDoseCount > 0`, not only at the ceiling: `MedsRecycleViewAdapter.java:199-201` hides it only when `latestDose == null || currentTotalDoseCount == 0 || nextExpiringDose == null`.
2. **PT-02 non-blocking** — that no code path compares `activeDoseCount` (or `pastDayDoseCount`) with `maxDose` other than for colour: `grep -n "getMaxDose()" pt/**` hits only `Med.java` (getter/validity/string), `MedsRecycleViewAdapter.java:232`, `MedActivity.java:268`, `EditMedActivity.java` (picker), and the JSON export. Confirm `EditDoseActivity.java:136-175` (save path) has no such check.
3. **PT-07 timestamp semantics** — `inventoryReportedAt` is in *seconds* (`Med.java:166-178` javadoc) and `getDoseCountBetween(db, id, reportedAt, now)` is inclusive at both ends (`DbHelper.java:455-456` `>=`/`<=`), so a dose logged in the same second as the count is subtracted. Decide the boundary before porting (Emivita would use `>` on `resolvedAtEpochMillis`).

---

#### 4. Cross-app synthesis (what to adopt, in order)

1. **Elapsed-since-last-dose + next-occurrence line on every medicine card, with a minute tick** (DT-03/DT-04/DT-05, PT-03/PT-04) — S–M, no schema change, the affordance all three apps converge on.
2. **PRN ceiling as a windowed sum with warn-don't-block and an "information, not advice" line** (PT-01/PT-02/PT-10) — M, two nullable columns.
3. **Public notification version + quiet lock-screen presentation option** (PT-06, DP-01/DP-03) — S + M, closes DP-F1 and gives the silent morning-pill use case.
4. **Reminder self-expiry until the next slot of the same reminder** (DP-02) — S, closes DT-F1; respect `lockedUntilTaken`.
5. **Missed-alarm detection with the two guards, and a "send a test reminder" button** (DP-04/DP-05) — S–M.
6. **`EveryNMonths` repeat pattern with anchor-relative arithmetic** (DT-09) — M.
7. **"Recount stock from last count"** (PT-07) — M, also fixes PT-F1.
8. **`Medicine.rxNumber`** (DT-10) — S.
9. Optional "window closed" alert per medicine (PT-05/PT-13) — M, only after 2.

Not recommended: photo proof (DT-08), refusing a dose at zero stock (DT-06), lazy boot receiver (DT-11), plaintext backups (DT-15/PT-15), 10-minute-window alarms for scheduled doses (PT-14).

### F.6.ONLINE — Dosage (Linux) · Medisafe / MyTherapy listings

#### Cross-app delta — ONLINE sources (Dosage · Medisafe · MyTherapy)

Date 2026-09-13. Read-only, online only (WebFetch / WebSearch / browser page-text); nothing downloaded. No local tree exists for any of the three apps, so the "(file:line)" column carries the URL plus the section, function or article read. Emivita side grepped in `C:/Users/User/Downloads/Builds/Emivita_reconciled (1)/Emivita/app/src/main/java/com/hazia/emivita/` (paths below are relative to that root). medTimer column from `medtimer_inventory.md`.

Delta only: rows are what the app does that neither Emivita nor medTimer has, does differently enough to matter, or does worse. Items where the app merely matches the Emivita+medTimer union are listed once under "covered by the union" at the end of each section, not as rows.

Caveat on sources. Dosage's README is seven feature bullets; the behaviour below comes from its release notes (`appdata.xml.in`), its `.blp` UI definitions and its GJS sources read on GitHub. Screenshots could not be read (image files). Medisafe's Google Play listing was read as page text; its help centre is `help-center.medisafe.com` (the `support.medisafe.com` host does not resolve). MyTherapy has no public help centre that resolves (`help.mytherapyapp.com` NXDOMAIN; `mytherapyapp.com/help` is a contact form), so its rows rest on the Play listing, the App Store listing and the homepage. Proprietary rows are therefore listing-level: they say *that* a feature exists and how the vendor describes it, not how it is implemented.

---

#### 1. Dosage (Linux, GTK4/libadwaita, GJS · github.com/diegopvlk/Dosage · GPL-3.0 · v2.1.8, 2026-06-22)

Sources read: README; `data/io.github.diegopvlk.Dosage.appdata.xml.in` (description + full release history v1.0.0→v2.1.8); `data/ui/med-dialog.blp`; `data/ui/preferences.blp`; `data/io.github.diegopvlk.Dosage.gschema.xml`; `src/window.js`; `src/medDialog.js`; `src/todayFactory.js`; `src/treatmentsFactory.js`; `src/treatmentsSorter.js`; `src/oneTimeDialog.js`; `src/editHistDialog.js`; `src/refillDialog.js`; `src/utils/helpers.js`; `src/utils/getHistoryExportFile.js`. Raw URLs: `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/diegopvlk/Dosage/main/<path>`.

Feature model in one paragraph (for orientation, not rows). A *treatment* has name, unit (free text, default "Pill(s)"), notes, colour (fixed palette classes), icon (65 symbolic SVGs), a list of `time + dose` rows, a frequency ∈ {Every Day, Specific Days, Day of Month, Cycle, As Needed}, an optional Duration (start/end dates), optional Inventory (current, "Remind Me At" threshold, refill amount), per-treatment "Increase Notification Priority", per-treatment "Recurring Notifications" with interval in minutes, and a per-treatment "Mark as Confirmed" switch. History entries carry `taken[1]` ∈ {−1 Missed, 0 Skipped, 1 Confirmed, 2 Auto-Confirmed, 3 Confirmed (Time Unknown)}. There is no interval/"every N hours" reminder, no linked/following dose, no expiration date, no weekend mode, no snooze (desktop notifications; renotify instead), no backup/restore beyond the JSON data file, no biometrics, no widgets. Preferences: autostart, auto-clear history (30 days), low-stock notification on/off, notification sound on/off, confirm/skip notification buttons on/off individually, export format.

---

#### 3. Behaviour cases (plain English; fresh tests, never ports)

**B1 — Missed doses are recorded for days the phone was off (DS-01).**
Given a medicine with a daily 08:00 fixed-time reminder and no dose events after Monday, and the device was powered off from Monday 09:00 until Thursday 07:00;
when the boot receiver runs `rescheduleAll()` on Thursday;
then History shows Tuesday 08:00 and Wednesday 08:00 as Missed rows for that medicine, the adherence card's denominator for the 30-day window includes them, stock is unchanged (sentinels stay at −1), and Thursday 08:00 is armed normally with no notification chain for the two past slots. A second boot on Thursday adds no further rows (idempotent on the unique `(reminderId, scheduledEpochMillis)` index).

**B2 — "Assume taken at day end" auto-resolve (DS-02).**
Given a reminder whose auto-resolve mode is *at day end* (not *at fire*);
when its 08:00 alarm fires;
then the dose is materialised as PENDING and notified like any other, and Skip remains available all day;
when the user does nothing and the day rolls over (or the app next runs after midnight);
then the dose becomes TAKEN with `resolvedBy = AUTO`, `resolvedAt` = the scheduled 08:00, stock is decremented exactly once, the History row and CSV read "Auto-confirmed", and a dose the user skipped at 10:00 stays SKIPPED.

**B3 — As-needed medicines are standing rows on Today (DS-10).**
Given a medicine flagged as-needed with no reminders and "show as-needed on Today" on;
when Today is opened;
then an "As needed" node appears above the timed nodes with a Take affordance and the last-used amount prefilled;
when Take is confirmed with amount 2;
then a TAKEN dose event with `reminderId = null`, amount 2 and `resolvedAt = now` exists, stock is decremented by 2, and the row remains on Today for further use; with the toggle off, the node is absent and the FAB path still works.

**B4 — Report export with a date range and a medicine filter (MK-03).**
Given 120 days of dose events for medicines A and B;
when the user exports a PDF for the last 90 days filtered to A;
then the document's subtitle names the range and the medicine, every row is A's and lies inside the range, the header states taken/skipped/missed totals for that filtered set, and the CSV twin has identical row count and the same seven columns; choosing "last 30 days, all medicines" reproduces today's export byte-for-byte except the subtitle.

**B5 — One low-stock digest and a tab count (DS-05).**
Given three medicines below their low-stock thresholds and one above;
when the 09:00 low-stock evaluation runs;
then exactly one notification lists the three by name (no per-medicine copies), the Medicines tab shows "3", refilling one of them clears its line and the badge reads "2" without a new notification, and the existing per-medicine `ONCE_WHEN_BELOW` semantics still hold for the `lastAlertNotifiedEpochDay` guard.

**B6 — Time-zone change asks, and honours the answer (MK-10).**
Given a fixed-time 08:00 reminder created in Europe/Rome and no zone anchor;
when the device zone changes to America/New_York;
then the next occurrence is 08:00 New York (today's silent behaviour, now explicit);
given the same reminder pinned to Europe/Rome;
then the next occurrence is 08:00 Rome = 02:00 New York, the Today row shows the local time with a "Rome" suffix, and interval / linked / daily-window reminders are unaffected either way (instant-based).

---

#### 4. Findings (defects in Emivita exposed by comparison)

**F1 — Adherence maths silently excludes never-fired doses (observation, not a code defect).** `core/scheduling/RealSchedulingCoordinator.kt:100-113` deliberately skips past slots when the floor is "now", and no other path materialises them; `feature/history/HistoryScreen.kt:370-383` and `AdherenceChart.kt:62-64` count "missed" only from PENDING rows that fired. Dosage (`window.js` `addMissedItems()`) treats those days as missed. Consequence: a week with the phone off reports as a week with no doses, and the 30-day adherence % rises. Design decision R60 is sound for *alarms* (no notification chain); the History side needs its own decision. No test pins either behaviour today.

**F2 — Export is bound to the 30-day display window, not a user range.** `feature/history/HistoryScreen.kt:132` builds `rangeStart` from `HISTORY_LOOKBACK_DAYS` and `:152` reuses those rows for both exports. The calendar view can browse older months but nothing can export them. Medisafe's help (9146319, 8103174) documents exactly this split and solves it with a report range. Limitation, not a bug; nothing in the code claims otherwise.

**F3 — From the proprietary listings and help pages: none.** Nothing on Medisafe's or MyTherapy's public pages describes behaviour that shows Emivita doing something *wrong*; where they differ Emivita is either ahead (MK-07a, MK-14, MK-15, MK-22) or has made an explicit exclusion (MK-02, MK-04, MK-17, MK-18, MK-19).

---

If either has it, the verdict stays excluded-by-design (online DB) but the evidence cell changes.

Open `https://www.mytherapyapp.com/` (section "Refill reminders") and the Play listing; if "prescription" turns out to be a synonym for refill-by-count, drop the `novel` half and mark the row `equal`.
